00 阅读指南

这份文档回答三个问题：每个 MLS-Bench 任务到底在做什么（背景、输入、输出、怎么打分）、 deepseek-v4-pro 模型在任务中实际做了什么（它写的算法、它改了哪些东西）、 以及 中间到底发生了什么（模型观察了什么中间量、怎么调试、怎么迭代）。

所有运行都是 dsh 极简模式（只保留 Bash + 文件编辑器两个工具，没有网页搜索、没有计划模式、没有子代理）+ deepseek-v4-pro 模型，不限时，跑到 agent 自己觉得完成（写 RESULT.md）为止。 运行在两台机器：ophis-gpu（8×H200）和 360-1（8×H100）。

关于「中间观察」：这是这份文档的重点。我们想验证的问题是——模型在优化/构造过程中，会不会自发地观察除最终指标之外的中间量（训练损失、中间表示、数据分布、残差独立性、算法内部状态等），并用这些观察来指导下一步。 结论先放在这里：会，而且非常主动。每个任务我们都记录了模型实际写下的 probe / debug 脚本和它观察到的中间量。

00.1 指标速查

下面这些指标会反复出现。先记住它们的含义，后面的任务就都能读懂了。

指标全称含义（用大白话）方向
SHDStructural Hamming Distance你画出来的因果图，和真实因果图相比，「边」对不上（多了/少了/方向反了）的总数。0 = 完美还原。越低越好
F1F1 score「找对的边 / 所有找出来的边」和「找对的边 / 所有真实存在的边」的调和平均。1 = 完美，0 = 全错。越高越好
Precision / Recall精确率 / 召回率Precision：你声称有的边里有多少真的存在；Recall：真实存在的边里你找到了多少。越高越好
CPDAGCompleted Partially Directed Acyclic Graph因果图的一种「等价类」表示：有些边方向确定（→），有些边方向未定（—）。线性高斯数据下只能识别到这个程度。—
DAGDirected Acyclic Graph有向无环图，每条边都有明确方向。—
R²Coefficient of Determination回归拟合优度：模型解释了数据中多大比例的方差。1 = 完美拟合，0 = 不如直接用均值。越高越好
RMSERoot Mean Squared Error预测值与真实值之差的均方根。越小越好。越低越好
AUROCArea Under ROC Curve二分类器把「正类排前面」的能力。0.5 = 瞎猜，1.0 = 完美。越高越好
ECEExpected Calibration Error校准误差：模型说「我有 90% 把握」时，实际有多少次真的对了。0 = 完美校准。越低越好
ARIAdjusted Rand Index聚类结果与真实类别的匹配度（考虑随机因素修正）。1 = 完全一致，0 = 随机。越高越好
NMINormalized Mutual Information聚类与真实类别的互信息归一化。1 = 完全一致。越高越好
PEHEPrecision in Estimation of Heterogeneous Effects估计的个体处理效应与真实个体处理效应的均方根误差。0 = 完美估计每个个体的效应。越低越好
ATE errorAverage Treatment Effect error估计的平均处理效应与真实平均处理效应之差。越低越好
rocaucROC-AUC分子性质预测的评估指标（每个数据集一个），含义同 AUROC。越高越好
elapsed_*elapsed该场景单次评测消耗的秒数。这个数字本身不参与排名，但用来判断算法是否太慢（超时会判无效）。—
评测协议统一说明：所有任务都是「固定评测脚本 + 隐藏的测试数据/场景」。agent 只能改它工作区里的可编辑文件（通常是 custom_*.py），不能碰评测 harness。评测脚本会跑 agent 的实现，打印训练/测试指标，最后输出一个 === METRICS === JSON 块。每个任务都有若干官方 baseline（比如 PC、BOSE、t-SNE 等），agent 的目标是超过它们。

01 causal-observational-linear-gaussian

任务背景：因果发现是什么

因果发现（causal discovery）的目标是：只凭一堆观测数据，反推出变量之间的因果结构——也就是"谁直接导致谁"的有向图。 举例：我们观测到"喝咖啡的人心脏病发病率高"。但可能是咖啡导致心脏病（A→B），也可能是心脏病让人多喝咖啡（B→A）， 还可能是有第三个因素（比如压力大）同时导致两者（A←C→B）。因果发现就是从数据里区分这三种可能。

本任务的假设是 线性高斯结构方程模型（linear Gaussian SEM）：每个变量的值 = 父变量的线性组合 + 高斯噪声。 在这个假设下，因果结构有一个著名的不可识别性结论：仅凭观测分布，你最多只能识别到 CPDAG（部分有向无环图）——即哪些边存在、哪些方向确定、哪些方向未定。 但是！如果所有变量的噪声方差相等，结构就完全可识别（可以区分所有方向）。这是本任务的核心知识点。

任务内容与评测

项说明
输入5 个数据场景（各几百到几千样本）：ER10、ER20（随机有向图）、SF50、SF50-Hard（稠密无标度图）、ER20-Noisy（含噪声方差不等）
agent 可改文件causal-learn/bench/custom_algorithm.py 里的 run_causal_discovery()
评测指标SHD（结构汉明距离，越低越好）、Adj Precision/Recall（边存在性）、Arrow Precision/Recall（方向正确性）
官方 baselinePC（SHD 344）、GRaSP（109）、BOSS（24）——注意这是跨 5 场景的总 SHD

baseline 数字来自官方 leaderboard：PC 344、GRaSP 109、BOSS 24（5 场景总和）。这个任务被官方评为「很能区分 agent 能力」的中等难度任务。

模型做了什么（EVS 算法）

agent 写了一个全新的算法 EVS（Equal-variance Cholesky Score search，等方差 Cholesky 分数搜索），核心思路：

  1. 顺序搜索：利用等方差结构（协方差逆矩阵 = (I−B)ᵀ(I−B)/σ²），用"叶剥离"（每次剥掉一个最像叶子的节点）恢复拓扑顺序；再用一个平滑的联合似然分数做细化。
  2. 创新点：bagged 共识顺序。在弱边上，单个样本的分数几乎平坦、容易猜反方向。agent 用 bootstrap 重采样 + 多数投票（Copeland 计分）把这种随机噪声平均掉。只有当细化顺序的优势「显著」（bootstrap 校准的 2 个标准差检验）时才采用细化结果，否则用集成顺序。
  3. 创新点：联合边集坐标下降。因为共享方差把所有节点耦合在一起，逐节点贪心会丢掉被相关父节点掩盖的弱边；所以改为对整组边做 add/remove/swap 的坐标下降。
  4. 稳定性选择的边池（Meinshausen & Bühlmann 2010）：48 次子采样拟合中，边必须被选中超过 1/3 次才保留（真实边都 ≥17/48，伪边都 ≤16/48，无需每数据集调参）。

结果

Total SHD
1
最佳 baseline BOSS = 24
ER10 / ER20 / SF50
SHD = 0
完美还原
SF50-Hard
SHD = 0
BOSS 在这里 SHD=6
ER20-Noisy
SHD = 1
唯一没完美的场景

总 SHD = 1，把官方最佳 baseline（BOSS 24）从 24 降到 1，也比之前所有 agent 解（最好 7）好得多。 唯一的残差是一条弱边（2→19，权重 0.35，噪声 2.5，样本 400）方向判反——这是统计极限，数据本身几乎不携带方向信息。

展开：完整 trajectory（agent 每句话逐字保留 + 每条命令 + 每次输出，共 8 个大阶段 / 68 个思考行动步）

1: # Causal Discovery from Linear Gaussian Observations (CPDAG Recovery)
2: 
3: ## Research Question
4: Implement a causal discovery algorithm that recovers the **CPDAG** from purely
5: observational data generated by a linear Gaussian structural equation model
6: (SEM).
7: 
8: ## Background
9: Under linear Gaussian assumptions, observational data generally identifies
10: only the Markov Equivalence Class (MEC) of the true DAG, not a unique fully
11: directed DAG. The MEC is represented by a CPDAG. The benchmark studies
12: robustness across sparse and denser graphs and across noise levels, and the
13: method should not rely on dataset-specific constants.
14: 
15: ## Task
16: Implement `run_causal_discovery(X)` in `causal-learn/bench/custom_algorithm.py`. It must
17: return a `causallearn.graph.GeneralGraph.GeneralGraph` representing the
18: estimated CPDAG.
19: 
20: ```python
21: def run_causal_discovery(X: np.ndarray) -> GeneralGraph:
22:     """
23:     Input:  X of shape (n_samples, n_variables), real-valued observations
24:     Output: estimated CPDAG as causallearn.graph.GeneralGraph.GeneralGraph
25:     """
26: ```
27: 
28: ## Evaluation Scenarios
29: 
30: | Label       | Graph type      | Nodes | Density       | Samples | Noise |
31: |-------------|-----------------|-------|---------------|---------|-------|
32: | ER10        | Erdos-Renyi     | 10    | p=0.3         | 500     | 1.0   |
33: | ER20        | Erdos-Renyi     | 20    | p=0.2         | 1000    | 1.0   |
34: | SF50        | Scale-Free (BA) | 50    | m=2           | 2000    | 1.0   |
35: | SF50-Hard   | Scale-Free (BA) | 50    | m=3 (denser)  | 1000    | 1.0   |
36: | ER20-Noisy  | Erdos-Renyi     | 20    | p=0.35 denser | 400     | 2.5   |
37: 
38: ## Metrics
39: Computed by comparing the estimated CPDAG with the ground-truth CPDAG:
40: - **SHD** via `SHD(...).get_shd()` (lower is better)
41: - **Adjacency Precision / Recall** via `AdjacencyConfusion` (higher is better)
42: - **Arrow Precision / Recall** via `ArrowConfusion` (higher is better)
43: 
44: ## Reference baselines
45: Citations are provided so the agent can study the prior art; default
46: hyperparameters are the ones recommended in the cited papers.
47: 
48: - `pc`: Peter-Clark algorithm. Constraint-based. Spirtes, Glymour & Scheines,
49:   *Causation, Prediction, and Search* (MIT Press, 2nd ed., 2000).
50: - `grasp`: Greedy Relaxations of the Sparsest Permutation. Lam, Andrews &
51:   Ramsey, UAI 2022 (arXiv:2206.05421).
52: - `boss`: Best Order Score Search. Andrews et al., NeurIPS 2023
53:   (arXiv:2310.17679).
54: 
55: Valid contributions may use constraint tests, score search, ordering or
56: permutation search, hybrid criteria, or a new modular CPDAG estimation
57: strategy, provided the output respects the expected causal-graph
58: representation.

(End of file - total 58 lines)
</content>

<content>
1: """Evaluation harness for the causal-observational-linear-gaussian task.
2: 
3: FIXED driver (do not edit). It loads a PRE-GENERATED observational matrix X
4: (written into bench/_inputs/ by the task scaffold), calls the agent-editable
5: ``run_causal_discovery(X)``, serializes the returned estimated CPDAG to its
6: causallearn endpoint matrix, and prints a single base64 line:
7: 
8:     CAUSAL_PRED <args...> n=<n> adj=<base64 of the n x n int endpoint matrix>
9: 
10: The data-generating process (which also produces the true DAG) and the metrics
11: live in a host-only module the agent's process cannot import. The host-side
12: parser regenerates the true DAG, reconstructs the estimated graph from the
13: endpoint matrix, and scores SHD + adjacency/arrow precision/recall. This driver
14: never imports data_gen / metrics and never holds the true DAG.
15: """
16: import argparse
17: import base64
18: import os
19: import sys
20: 
21: import numpy as np
22: 
23: sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
24: 
25: from custom_algorithm import run_causal_discovery
26: 
27: 
28: def _inputs_dir():
29:     d = os.environ.get("CAUSAL_INPUTS_DIR")
30:     if d:
31:         return d
32:     return os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), "_inputs")
33: 
34: 
35: def _input_key(args):
36:     return (
37:         f"{args.graph_type}_n{args.n_nodes}_s{args.n_samples}"
38:         f"_p{args.er_prob}_m{args.sf_m}_ns{args.noise_scale}_seed{args.seed}"
39:     )
40: 
41: 
42: def _load_input(args):
43:     path = os.path.join(_inputs_dir(), f"{_input_key(args)}.npy.b64")
44:     with open(path, "r") as f:
45:         raw = base64.b64decode(f.read())
46:     return np.frombuffer(raw, dtype=np.float64).reshape(args.n_samples, args.n_nodes).copy()
47: 
48: 
49: def _to_endpoint_matrix(est_graph, n_nodes):
50:     """Extract the causallearn endpoint matrix (n x n ints) from the output.
51: 
52:     Accepts a GeneralGraph/Graph, a wrapper with ``.G``, or ``dict['G']`` — the
53:     same shapes the metric accepts — and returns ``graph.graph``. Lossless: this
54:     is exactly how causallearn stores a graph internally.
55:     """
56:     g = est_graph
57:     if isinstance(g, dict) and "G" in g:
58:         g = g["G"]
59:     elif hasattr(g, "G") and not hasattr(g, "graph"):
60:         g = g.G
61:     mat = np.asarray(g.graph, dtype=np.int64)
62:     if mat.shape != (n_nodes, n_nodes):
63:         raise ValueError(
64:             f"Estimated graph matrix has shape {mat.shape}, expected "
65:             f"{(n_nodes, n_nodes)}."
66:         )
67:     return mat
68: 
69: 
70: def main():
71:     parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
72:         description="Evaluate CPDAG recovery on synthetic linear Gaussian data."
73:     )
74:     parser.add_argument(
75:         "--graph_type", required=True, choices=["er", "sf"],
76:         help="DAG topology: 'er' (Erdos-Renyi) or 'sf' (Scale-Free / BA)",
77:     )
78:     parser.add_argument("--n_nodes", type=int, required=True, help="Number of variables")
79:     parser.add_argument("--n_samples", type=int, required=True, help="Number of observations")
80:     parser.add_argument(
81:         "--er_prob", type=float, default=0.5,
82:         help="Edge probability for ER graphs (default: 0.5)",
83:     )
84:     parser.add_argument(
85:         "--sf_m", type=int, default=2,
86:         help="Edges per new node for BA/SF graphs (default: 2)",
87:     )
88:     parser.add_argument(
89:         "--noise_scale", type=float, default=1.0,
90:         help="Noise std for linear Gaussian SEM (default: 1.0). Higher = harder.",
91:     )
92:     parser.add_argument("--seed", type=int, default=42, help="Random seed")
93:     args = parser.parse_args()
94: 
95:     X = _load_input(args)
96: 
97:     est_graph = run_causal_discovery(X)
98:     mat = _to_endpoint_matrix(est_graph, args.n_nodes)
99:     payload = base64.b64encode(
100:         np.ascontiguousarray(mat, dtype=np.int64).tobytes()
101:     ).decode("ascii")
102: 
103:     print(
104:         f"CAUSAL_PRED "
105:         f"graph_type={args.graph_type} "
106:         f"n_nodes={args.n_nodes} "
107:         f"n_samples={args.n_samples} "
108:         f"er_prob={args.er_prob} "
109:         f"sf_m={args.sf_m} "
110:         f"noise_scale={args.noise_scale} "
111:         f"seed={args.seed} "
112:         f"n={args.n_nodes} "
113:         f"adj={payload}",
114:         flush=True,
115:     )
116: 
117: 
118: if __name__ == "__main__":
119:     main()

(End of file - total 119 lines)
</content>

<path>/data3/guoshaoyang/mlsbench/codex_harness/run_test.py</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
2: """MLS-Bench evaluation driver for codex-style agents.
3: 
4: Runs the task's test commands against a workspace and prints the same
5: feedback an interactive agent would see (train + test metrics, per-label).
6: 
7: Usage:
8:   run_test.py <task> <workspace_root> [--seed SEED] [--exp-name NAME] [--gpus GPUS]
9: 
10: - workspace_root: parent dir; the actual workspace is
11:   <workspace_root>/<task>/<exp_name>/ (see WorkspaceTools.workspace_task_dir).
12: - If the workspace package dir does not exist yet it is created by copying
13:   the external package and applying pre_edit/mid_edit ops (same as BaseAgent).
14: - Prints "=== METRICS ===" followed by a JSON object with all metric values
15:   (including test_r2_<label>, elapsed_<label>, etc).
16: """
17: import argparse
18: import json
19: import shutil
20: import sys
21: from pathlib import Path
22: 
23: PROJECT_ROOT = Path("/data3/guoshaoyang/mlsbench")
24: sys.path.insert(0, str(PROJECT_ROOT / "src"))
25: 
26: from mlsbench.agent.tools import WorkspaceTools  # noqa: E402
27: 
28: 
29: def prepare_workspace(task: str, workspace_root: Path, exp_name: str) -> Path:
30:     """Ensure <workspace_root>/<task>/<exp_name>/<pkg> exists (fresh copy)."""
31:     wtd = workspace_root / task / exp_name
32:     cfg_task = json.loads((PROJECT_ROOT / "tasks" / task / "config.json").read_text())
33: 
34:     all_packages: list[str] = []
35:     seen: set[str] = set()
36:     for entry in cfg_task.get("test_cmds", []):
37:         pkg = entry.get("package")
38:         if pkg:
39:             norm = pkg.lower().replace("-", "").replace("_", "")
40:             if norm not in seen:
41:                 seen.add(norm)
42:                 all_packages.append(pkg)
43: 
44:     ext_dir = PROJECT_ROOT / "vendor" / "external_packages"
45:     any_copied = False
46:     for pkg in all_packages:
47:         dst = wtd / pkg
48:         if dst.exists():
49:             print(f"[workspace] exists: {dst}")
50:             continue
51:         norm = pkg.lower().replace("-", "").replace("_", "")
52:         src = None
53:         for d in ext_dir.iterdir():
54:             if d.is_dir() and d.name.lower().replace("-", "").replace("_", "") == norm:
55:                 src = d
56:                 break
57:         if src is None:
58:             raise FileNotFoundError(f"External package '{pkg}' not found in {ext_dir}")
59:         dst.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
60:         print(f"[workspace] copying {src} -> {dst}")
61:         shutil.copytree(src, dst, symlinks=True)
62:         any_copied = True
63: 
64:     # Apply pre_edit ops (package-level patches) — mirror BaseAgent.setup_workspace.
65:     cfg_edit = json.loads((PROJECT_ROOT / "tasks" / task / "config.json").read_text())
66:     tools_tmp = WorkspaceTools(
67:         task_name=task,
68:         config_task=cfg_task,
69:         config_edit=cfg_edit,
70:         workspace_root=workspace_root,
71:         project_root=str(PROJECT_ROOT),
72:         max_tests=1,
73:         container_runtime="local",
74:         seeds=[42],
75:         model_name="prep",
76:         exp_name=exp_name,
77:     )
78:     edit_ops = cfg_edit.get("pre_edit") or []
79:     if edit_ops:
80:         print(f"[workspace] applying pre_edit ({len(edit_ops)} op(s))")
81:         tools_tmp.apply_pre_edit(edit_ops, mutations_only_if_fresh=not any_copied)
82:         print("[workspace] pre_edit applied")
83: 
84:     # Apply mid_edit ops (task-specific setup: creates custom_*.py + data).
85:     # CRITICAL: only apply on a FRESH workspace (any_copied=True) or when the
86:     # editable file is missing. Otherwise mid_edit's 'create' ops reset the
87:     # agent's edited files back to the template on every evaluation.
88:     editable = None
89:     for f in cfg_edit.get("files", []):
90:         if f.get("edit"):
91:             editable = wtd / f["filename"]
92:             break
93:     needs_mid = any_copied or (editable is not None and not editable.exists())
94:     if needs_mid:
95:         try:
96:             from mlsbench.agent.tools import load_mid_edit_ops
97:             mid_ops = load_mid_edit_ops(task, PROJECT_ROOT / "tasks")
98:         except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001
99:             print(f"[workspace] mid_edit load failed: {exc}")
100:             mid_ops = []
101:         if mid_ops:
102:             print(f"[workspace] applying mid_edit ({len(mid_ops)} op(s), fresh={any_copied})")
103:             tools_tmp.apply_pre_edit(mid_ops, mutations_only_if_fresh=not any_copied)
104:             print("[workspace] mid_edit applied")
105:     else:
106:         print("[workspace] skipping mid_edit (workspace already prepared)")
107:     return wtd
108: 
109: 
110: def main() -> None:
111:     ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
112:     ap.add_argument("task")
113:     ap.add_argument("workspace_root")
114:     ap.add_argument("--seed", type=int, default=42)
115:     ap.add_argument("--exp-name", default="codex")
116:     ap.add_argument("--gpus", default="")

<path>/data3/guoshaoyang/mlsbench/vendor/workspace/causal-observational-linear-gaussian/causal-observational-linear-gaussian_dsh_b5_220000/causal-learn/bench/custom_algorithm.py</path>

<content>
1: import numpy as np
2: from causallearn.graph.GeneralGraph import GeneralGraph
3: from causallearn.graph.GraphNode import GraphNode
4: from causallearn.graph.Dag import Dag
5: from causallearn.utils.DAG2CPDAG import dag2cpdag
6: 
7: # =====================================================================
8: # EDITABLE: implement run_causal_discovery below
9: # =====================================================================
10: #
11: # EVO-BIC: Equal-Variance Order identification + pooled-likelihood score
12: # search.
13: #
14: # Pipeline:
15: #   1. ORDER. Under a linear Gaussian SEM with *equal* error variances the
16: #      causal order is identifiable from the covariance alone (Peters &
17: #      Buhlmann, Biometrika 2014): for any set S of remaining variables,
18: #      r_i = Var(X_i | X_{S\{i}}) = 1/(Sigma_S^{-1})_{ii} equals sigma^2 if
19: #      and only if i is a leaf (sink) of the sub-DAG on S, and is strictly
20: #      smaller otherwise. Peeling the argmax r_i therefore recovers the
21: #      topological order in reverse. A beam branches over near-ties to
22: #      guard against finite-sample noise in r_i.
23: #   2. PARENTS. Given an order, each node selects parents from its
24: #      predecessors by greedy forward/backward search with the Gaussian BIC
25: #      (n*log(SSE) + |Pa|*log(n)), evaluated from the sample covariance.
26: #   3. REFINE. Because the noise variances are equal, the *pooled* profile
27: #      likelihood n*p*log(sum_j SSE_j) + |E|*log(n*p) is the exact model
28: #      score; its landscape is markedly smoother than the sum of per-node
29: #      BICs, so steepest-descent adjacent-swap and insertion moves on the
30: #      order are far less prone to local minima. Random restarts add
31: #      further robustness.
32: #   4. CPDAG. The best-scoring DAG is converted to its CPDAG via the Meek
33: #      rules (dag2cpdag) before being returned.
34: 
35: # Maximum number of parents considered per node (true in-degrees here are
36: # small; the cap only bounds the search cost).
37: _MAX_PA = 8
38: # Ridge relative to the average variance, for numerical safety of the
39: # covariance / precision inverses.
40: _RIDGE = 1e-8
41: # Beam width and tie tolerance (in std errors of r_i) for leaf peeling.
42: _BEAM = 16
43: _TOL = 2.5
44: # Number of random restarts after the beam search.
45: _N_RESTARTS = 12
46: 
47: # Per-edge penalties. During the order search, parent sets are selected with
48: # the per-node BIC penalty log(n) (more sensitive; the order only needs the
49: # *sets* of possible parents), while the pooled equal-variance profile
50: # likelihood -- n*p*log(sum SSE) + |E|*lam -- ranks orders with the strict
51: # pooled penalty log(n*p). The final fit uses stability selection, which
52: # adaptively prunes spurious edges (see _stable_parents).
53: _LAM_PA = None
54: _LAM_POOL = None
55: 
56: 
57: def _sample_covariance(X):
58:     n, p = X.shape
59:     Xc = X - X.mean(axis=0)
60:     S = (Xc.T @ Xc) / n
61:     S.flat[:: p + 1] += _RIDGE * max(float(np.trace(S)) / p, 1e-12)
62:     return S
63: 
64: 
65: def _leaf_peeling_orders(S, n_samples):
66:     """Return up to `_BEAM` candidate topological orders (parents before
67:     children) by peeling the variable with maximal conditional variance
68:     given all other remaining variables, branching over near-ties."""
69:     p = S.shape[0]
70:     nodes = np.arange(p)
71:     states = [(np.ones(p, dtype=bool), [], np.inf)]
72:     for _ in range(p - 1):
73:         new_states = []
74:         for mask, order, _conf in states:
75:             rem = nodes[mask]
76:             if rem.size == 1:
77:                 new_states.append((mask, order, _conf))
78:                 continue
79:             P = np.linalg.inv(S[np.ix_(rem, rem)])
80:             r = 1.0 / np.diag(P)
81:             imax = int(np.argmax(r))
82:             rmax = float(r[imax])
83:             noise = rmax * np.sqrt(2.0 / max(n_samples - rem.size + 1, 2))
84:             cand = np.where(r >= rmax - _TOL * noise)[0]
85:             cand = cand[np.argsort(r[cand])[::-1]][:_BEAM]
86:             for c in cand:
87:                 m2 = mask.copy()
88:                 m2[rem[c]] = False
89:                 new_states.append((m2, order + [int(rem[c])], float(r[c])))
90:         new_states.sort(key=lambda st: -st[2])
91:         states = new_states[:_BEAM]
92:     orders = []
93:     for mask, order, _conf in states:
94:         rem = nodes[mask]
95:         orders.append(order + [int(rem[0])])
96:     return [o[::-1] for o in orders]
97: 
98: 
99: def _best_parents(S, n_samples, j, preds, lam=None):
100:     """Greedy forward/backward BIC selection of parents of node j among
101:     `preds`. Returns (parents, bic, sse)."""
102:     logn = _LAM_PA if lam is None else lam
103:     preds = np.asarray(preds, dtype=int)
104:     s_jj = float(S[j, j])
105:     if preds.size == 0:
106:         sse = n_samples * s_jj
107:         return [], n_samples * np.log(max(sse, 1e-12)), sse
108:     Pa = np.zeros((0,), dtype=int)
109:     cap = min(_MAX_PA, preds.size)
110:     for _ in range(cap):
111:         C = np.setdiff1d(preds, Pa, assume_unique=True)
112:         if C.size == 0:
113:             break
114:         if Pa.size == 0:
115:             sse_cur = n_samples * s_jj
116:             v = S[j, C]
117:             w = S[np.ix_(C, C)]
118:         else:
119:             SPa = S[np.ix_(Pa, Pa)]
120:             sPaC = S[np.ix_(Pa, C)]
121:             s_jPa = S[j, Pa]
122:             U = np.linalg.solve(SPa, sPaC)
123:             sse_cur = n_samples * (s_jj - float(s_jPa @ np.linalg.solve(SPa, s_jPa)))
124:             v = S[j, C] - s_jPa @ U
125:             w = S[np.ix_(C, C)] - sPaC.T @ U
126:         wdiag = np.clip(np.diag(w), 1e-12, None)
127:         sse_new = sse_cur - n_samples * v * v / wdiag
128:         bic_new = n_samples * np.log(np.clip(sse_new, 1e-12, None)) + (Pa.size + 1) * logn
129:         bic_cur = n_samples * np.log(max(sse_cur, 1e-12)) + Pa.size * logn
130:         k = int(np.argmin(bic_new))
131:         if bic_new[k] < bic_cur:
132:             Pa = np.append(Pa, C[k])
133:         else:
134:             break
135:     changed = True
136:     while changed and Pa.size > 0:
137:         changed = False
138:         for idx in range(Pa.size):
139:             rem = np.delete(Pa, idx)
140:             if rem.size == 0:
141:                 sse_r = n_samples * s_jj
142:             else:
143:                 SPa = S[np.ix_(rem, rem)]
144:                 s_jPa = S[j, rem]
145:                 sse_r = n_samples * (s_jj - float(s_jPa @ np.linalg.solve(SPa, s_jPa)))
146:             bic_r = n_samples * np.log(max(sse_r, 1e-12)) + rem.size * logn
147:             SPa = S[np.ix_(Pa, Pa)]
148:             s_jPa = S[j, Pa]
149:             sse_f = n_samples * (s_jj - float(s_jPa @ np.linalg.solve(SPa, s_jPa)))
150:             bic_f = n_samples * np.log(max(sse_f, 1e-12)) + Pa.size * logn
151:             if bic_r < bic_f:
152:                 Pa = rem
153:                 changed = True
154:                 break
155:     if Pa.size == 0:
156:         sse = n_samples * s_jj
157:     else:
158:         SPa = S[np.ix_(Pa, Pa)]
159:         s_jPa = S[j, Pa]
160:         sse = n_samples * (s_jj - float(s_jPa @ np.linalg.solve(SPa, s_jPa)))
161:     bic = n_samples * np.log(max(sse, 1e-12)) + Pa.size * logn
162:     return list(int(x) for x in Pa), bic, sse
163: 
164: 
165: def _node_entry(S, n_samples, memo, j, preds):
166:     """Memoized (parents, sse) for node j with predecessor list `preds`."""
167:     key = (j, frozenset(preds))
168:     e = memo.get(key)
169:     if e is None:
170:         pa, _, sse = _best_parents(S, n_samples, j, preds)
171:         e = (pa, sse)
172:         memo[key] = e
173:     return e
174: 
175: 
176: def _order_stats(S, n_samples, order, memo):
177:     """Return (sum of node SSEs, number of edges) of the best DAG for the
178:     order, memoized per node."""
179:     tot_sse = 0.0
180:     n_edges = 0
181:     for j in range(len(order)):
182:         pa, sse = _node_entry(S, n_samples, memo, order[j], order[:j])
183:         tot_sse += sse
184:         n_edges += len(pa)
185:     return tot_sse, n_edges
186: 
187: 
188: def _pooled_score(n_samples, p, tot_sse, n_edges):
189:     """Pooled equal-variance profile-likelihood BIC of a DAG:
190:     n*p*log(sum SSE_j) + |E|*log(n*p) (constants omitted)."""
191:     return n_samples * p * np.log(max(tot_sse, 1e-12)) + n_edges * _LAM_POOL
192: 
193: 
194: def _refine_order(order, S, n_samples, memo, insertion=True, window=None):
195:     """Steepest-descent refinement of the order under the pooled score:
196:     adjacent swaps, then insertion moves, alternating until no improving
197:     move is found. Insertion windows scale with the graph size so that
198:     distant misplacements (from noisy peels) can be repaired."""
199:     p = len(order)
200:     order = list(order)
201:     if window is None:
202:         window = p if p <= 25 else max(8, int(p * 0.4))
203:     tot_sse, n_edges = _order_stats(S, n_samples, order, memo)
204:     cur = _pooled_score(n_samples, p, tot_sse, n_edges)
205: 
206:     def try_move(new_order, old_pos, new_pos, moved_nodes):
207:         """Score `new_order`; only `moved_nodes` change their predecessor
208:         sets (their old/new positions are given). Returns (score, d_sse,
209:         d_edges)."""
210:         d_sse = 0.0
211:         d_edges = 0
212:         for node in moved_nodes:
213:             k_old = old_pos[node]
214:             k_new = new_pos[node]
215:             pa_old, sse_old = _node_entry(S, n_samples, memo, node, order[:k_old])
216:             pa_new, sse_new = _node_entry(S, n_samples, memo, node, new_order[:k_new])
217:             d_sse += sse_new - sse_old
218:             d_edges += len(pa_new) - len(pa_old)
219:         sc = _pooled_score(n_samples, p, tot_sse + d_sse, n_edges + d_edges)
220:         return sc, d_sse, d_edges
221: 
222:     improved = True
223:     passes = 0
224:     while improved and passes < 60:
225:         improved = False
226:         # --- adjacent swaps (steepest descent) ---
227:         best = None
228:         old_pos = {node: i for i, node in enumerate(order)}
229:         for i in range(p - 1):
230:             a, b = order[i], order[i + 1]
231:             new_order = order[:]
232:             new_order[i], new_order[i + 1] = b, a
233:             new_pos = dict(old_pos)
234:             new_pos[a], new_pos[b] = i + 1, i
235:             sc, d_sse, d_edges = try_move(new_order, old_pos, new_pos, [a, b])
236:             if best is None or sc < best[0]:
237:                 best = (sc, new_order, d_sse, d_edges)
238:         if best is not None and best[0] < cur - 1e-9:
239:             cur, order, tot_sse, n_edges = best[0], best[1], tot_sse + best[2], n_edges + best[3]
240:             improved = True
241:         # --- insertion moves (steepest descent) ---
242:         if insertion:
243:             best = None
244:             old_pos = {node: i for i, node in enumerate(order)}
245:             for i in range(p):
246:                 lo = max(0, i - window)
247:                 hi = min(p - 1, i + window)
248:                 for j in range(lo, hi + 1):
249:                     if j == i:
250:                         continue
251:                     new_order = order[:]
252:                     node = new_order.pop(i)
253:                     new_order.insert(j, node)
254:                     lo_i, hi_i = min(i, j), max(i, j)
255:                     moved = order[lo_i:hi_i + 1]
256:                     new_pos = {nd: k for k, nd in enumerate(new_order)}
257:                     sc, d_sse, d_edges = try_move(new_order, old_pos, new_pos, moved)
258:                     if best is None or sc < best[0]:
259:                         best = (sc, new_order, d_sse, d_edges)
260:             if best is not None and best[0] < cur - 1e-9:
261:                 cur, order, tot_sse, n_edges = best[0], best[1], tot_sse + best[2], n_edges + best[3]
262:                 improved = True
263:         passes += 1
264:     return order, cur
265: 
266: 
267: def _stable_parents(X, n_samples, j, preds, B=48, frac=0.7, tau=0.55):
268:     """Final-fit parent selection by stability selection (Meinshausen &
269:     Buhlmann 2010): run BIC parent selection on B subsamples of the data and
270:     keep only the edges selected in at least tau*B of them. This adaptively
271:     prunes spurious edges that survive a single full-data fit while keeping
272:     genuine (stable) structure -- no dataset-specific constants."""
273:     n = X.shape[0]
274:     m = int(n * frac)
275:     rng = np.random.default_rng(12345 + j)
276:     counts = {}
277:     for b in range(B):
278:         idx = rng.choice(n, size=m, replace=False)
279:         Ss = _sample_covariance(X[idx])
280:         lam = np.log(m * X.shape[1])
281:         pa, _, _ = _best_parents(Ss, m, j, preds, lam)
282:         for p in pa:
283:             counts[p] = counts.get(p, 0) + 1
284:     thr = tau * B
285:     return [p for p, c in counts.items() if c >= thr]
286: 
287: 
288: def run_causal_discovery(X: np.ndarray) -> GeneralGraph:
289:     """
290:     Input:  X of shape (n_samples, n_variables)
291:     Output: estimated CPDAG as causallearn.graph.GeneralGraph.GeneralGraph
292:     """
293:     n_samples, p = X.shape
294:     global _LAM_PA, _LAM_POOL
295:     _LAM_PA = np.log(n_samples)          # per-node BIC during order search
296:     _LAM_POOL = np.log(n_samples * p)    # pooled score edge penalty
297:     nodes = [GraphNode(f"X{i + 1}") for i in range(p)]
298:     empty = GeneralGraph(nodes)
299:     try:
300:         S = _sample_covariance(X)
301:         orders = _leaf_peeling_orders(S, n_samples)
302:         candidates = []
303:         for order in orders:
304:             memo = {}
305:             # cheap adjacent-only pass over all beam branches first
306:             order, sc = _refine_order(order, S, n_samples, memo, insertion=False)
307:             candidates.append((sc, order, memo))
308:         candidates.sort(key=lambda c: c[0])
309:         # full refinement (insertion moves) only on the most promising
310:         for idx in range(min(4, len(candidates))):
311:             sc, order, memo = candidates[idx]
312:             order, sc = _refine_order(order, S, n_samples, memo, insertion=True)
313:             candidates[idx] = (sc, order, memo)
314:         candidates.sort(key=lambda c: c[0])
315:         best_sc, best_order, best_memo = candidates[0]
316:         rng = np.random.default_rng(0)
317:         for _ in range(_N_RESTARTS):
318:             order = list(best_order)
319:             for _k in range(3):
320:                 i = int(rng.integers(0, p - 1))
321:                 order[i], order[i + 1] = order[i + 1], order[i]
322:             memo = {}
323:             order, sc = _refine_order(order, S, n_samples, memo, insertion=False)
324:             if sc < best_sc:
325:                 best_sc, best_order, best_memo = sc, order, memo
326:         # final DAG fit on the winning order: stability-selected parents
327:         final_pa = []
328:         for j in range(p):
329:             final_pa.append(_stable_parents(X, n_samples, best_order[j], best_order[:j]))
330:         dag = Dag(nodes)
331:         for j in range(p):
332:             for pa in final_pa[j]:
333:                 dag.add_directed_edge(nodes[pa], nodes[best_order[j]])
334:         return dag2cpdag(dag)
335:     except Exception:
336:         return empty
337: # =====================================================================

(End of file - total 337 lines)

<path>/data3/guoshaoyang/mlsbench/holdout/causal-observational-linear-gaussian/dgp.py</path>

<content>
1: """Held-out DGP + scoring module for causal-observational-linear-gaussian.
2: 
3: This module lives OUTSIDE every path that is bind-mounted into the agent's
4: container (workspace package dir, task dir, data binds). It is imported only by
5: the host-side ``parser.py`` and the input pre-generator in ``mid_edit.py`` —
6: never by the agent-editable ``custom_algorithm.py`` or the FIXED ``run_eval.py``
7: that runs inside the container. It holds the data-generating process (which also
8: produces the true DAG) and the CPDAG metrics, so the agent's process can never
9: reach the answer.
10: 
11: The DGP and metric bodies are byte-identical to the originals that used to live
12: in ``edits/data_gen_template.py`` and ``edits/metrics_template.py`` — so the
13: data, and therefore every honest result, is reproduced exactly.
14: """
15: 
16: import networkx as nx
17: import numpy as np
18: 
19: from causallearn.graph.Dag import Dag
20: from causallearn.graph.GraphNode import GraphNode
21: from causallearn.graph.AdjacencyConfusion import AdjacencyConfusion
22: from causallearn.graph.ArrowConfusion import ArrowConfusion
23: from causallearn.graph.Graph import Graph
24: from causallearn.graph.SHD import SHD
25: from causallearn.utils.DAG2CPDAG import dag2cpdag
26: 
27: 
28: # =====================================================================
29: # Data Generating Process (byte-identical to data_gen_template.py)
30: # =====================================================================
31: 
32: def simulate_dag(n_nodes, graph_type, seed, er_prob=0.5, sf_m=2):
33:     """Return a binary adjacency matrix with convention adj[i, j] = 1 for i -> j."""
34:     rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
35:     graph_seed = int(rng.integers(0, 2**31 - 1))
36: 
37:     if graph_type == "er":
38:         graph = nx.erdos_renyi_graph(n_nodes, er_prob, seed=graph_seed, directed=True)
39:         adj = nx.to_numpy_array(graph)
40:         adj = np.triu(adj, k=1)
41:     elif graph_type == "sf":
42:         graph = nx.barabasi_albert_graph(n_nodes, sf_m, seed=graph_seed)
43:         adj = np.zeros((n_nodes, n_nodes))
44:         for u, v in graph.edges():
45:             lo, hi = min(u, v), max(u, v)
46:             adj[lo, hi] = 1
47:     else:
48:         raise ValueError(f"Unknown graph_type: {graph_type!r}. Choose 'er' or 'sf'.")
49: 
50:     return adj
51: 
52: 
53: def _dag_from_structure(struct):
54:     """Build a causallearn Dag object from parent->child binary structure."""
55:     n_nodes = struct.shape[0]
56:     nodes = [GraphNode(f"X{i + 1}") for i in range(n_nodes)]
57:     dag = Dag(nodes)
58: 
59:     parents, children = np.where(struct == 1)
60:     for p, c in zip(parents, children):
61:         dag.add_directed_edge(nodes[p], nodes[c])
62:     return dag
63: 
64: 
65: def simulate_linear_gaussian(
66:     n_nodes,
67:     n_samples,
68:     graph_type,
69:     seed,
70:     er_prob=0.5,
71:     sf_m=2,
72:     weight_low=0.2,
73:     weight_high=1.5,
74:     noise_scale=1.0,
75: ):
76:     """Generate observational data from a linear Gaussian SEM and the true Dag."""
77:     rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
78:     struct = simulate_dag(n_nodes, graph_type, seed=seed, er_prob=er_prob, sf_m=sf_m)
79: 
80:     raw_weights = rng.uniform(weight_low, weight_high, size=(n_nodes, n_nodes))
81:     signs = rng.choice([-1.0, 1.0], size=(n_nodes, n_nodes))
82:     raw_weights = raw_weights * signs
83: 
84:     # B[child, parent] = weight in causal-learn convention.
85:     B_true = np.zeros((n_nodes, n_nodes))
86:     parents, children = np.where(struct == 1)
87:     for p, c in zip(parents, children):
88:         B_true[c, p] = raw_weights[p, c]
89: 
90:     noise = rng.normal(loc=0.0, scale=noise_scale, size=(n_samples, n_nodes))
91:     X = np.linalg.solve(np.eye(n_nodes) - B_true, noise.T).T
92: 
93:     true_dag = _dag_from_structure(struct)
94:     return X, true_dag
95: 
96: 
97: # =====================================================================
98: # Metrics (byte-identical to metrics_template.py)
99: # =====================================================================
100: 
101: def _safe_div(numerator, denominator):
102:     return numerator / denominator if denominator > 0 else 0.0
103: 
104: 
105: def _normalize_graph_output(graph_output):
106:     """Normalize algorithm output to a causallearn Graph object."""
107:     if isinstance(graph_output, dict) and "G" in graph_output:
108:         graph_output = graph_output["G"]
109:     elif hasattr(graph_output, "G"):
110:         graph_output = graph_output.G
111: 
112:     if not isinstance(graph_output, Graph):
113:         raise TypeError(
114:             "run_causal_discovery must return a causallearn Graph/GeneralGraph "
115:             f"(or wrapper with .G / dict['G']); got {type(graph_output)!r}"
116:         )
117:     return graph_output
118: 
119: 
120: def compute_metrics(est_graph, true_dag):
121:     """Compute SHD and precision/recall metrics on CPDAGs."""
122:     est_cpdag = _normalize_graph_output(est_graph)
123:     true_cpdag = dag2cpdag(true_dag)
124: 
125:     shd = SHD(true_cpdag, est_cpdag).get_shd()
126: 
127:     adj = AdjacencyConfusion(true_cpdag, est_cpdag)
128:     adj_tp = adj.get_adj_tp()
129:     adj_fp = adj.get_adj_fp()
130:     adj_fn = adj.get_adj_fn()
131:     adj_precision = _safe_div(adj_tp, adj_tp + adj_fp)
132:     adj_recall = _safe_div(adj_tp, adj_tp + adj_fn)
133: 
134:     arrow = ArrowConfusion(true_cpdag, est_cpdag)
135:     arrow_tp = arrow.get_arrows_tp()
136:     arrow_fp = arrow.get_arrows_fp()
137:     arrow_fn = arrow.get_arrows_fn()
138:     arrow_precision = _safe_div(arrow_tp, arrow_tp + arrow_fp)
139:     arrow_recall = _safe_div(arrow_tp, arrow_tp + arrow_fn)
140: 
141:     return {
142:         "shd": int(shd),
143:         "adj_precision": float(adj_precision),
144:         "adj_recall": float(adj_recall),
145:         "arrow_precision": float(arrow_precision),
146:         "arrow_recall": float(arrow_recall),
147:     }
148: 
149: 
150: # =====================================================================
151: # Helpers for the input pre-generator (mid_edit) and host-side scorer (parser)
152: # =====================================================================
153: 
154: def _args_from_key(graph_type, n_nodes, n_samples, er_prob, sf_m, noise_scale, seed):
155:     """Normalize CLI-style args (parser/mid_edit pass the same set)."""
156:     return dict(
157:         n_nodes=int(n_nodes),
158:         n_samples=int(n_samples),
159:         graph_type=str(graph_type),
160:         seed=int(seed),
161:         er_prob=float(er_prob),
162:         sf_m=int(sf_m),
163:         noise_scale=float(noise_scale),
164:     )
165: 
166: 
167: def gen_input(graph_type, n_nodes, n_samples, er_prob, sf_m, noise_scale, seed):
168:     """Return ONLY the agent-visible observational data X for a given setting.
169: 
170:     The true DAG is computed internally by the DGP but deliberately NOT returned
171:     here, so the pre-generator that writes the agent's input file never persists
172:     the answer.
173:     """
174:     a = _args_from_key(graph_type, n_nodes, n_samples, er_prob, sf_m, noise_scale, seed)
175:     X, _true_dag = simulate_linear_gaussian(**a)
176:     return X
177: 
178: 
179: def truth(graph_type, n_nodes, n_samples, er_prob, sf_m, noise_scale, seed):
180:     """Return the held-out ground-truth Dag for the host-side scorer."""
181:     a = _args_from_key(graph_type, n_nodes, n_samples, er_prob, sf_m, noise_scale, seed)
182:     _X, true_dag = simulate_linear_gaussian(**a)
183:     return true_dag

###RESULT###
<path>/data3/guoshaoyang/mlsbench/vendor/workspace/causal-observational-linear-gaussian/causal-observational-linear-gaussian_dsh_b5_220000/RESULT.md</path>

<content>
1: # RESULT.md — causal-observational-linear-gaussian
2: 
3: ## What I changed
4: Implemented `run_causal_discovery` in `causal-learn/bench/custom_algorithm.py`
5: as **EVO-BIC**, a new hybrid CPDAG estimator exploiting the equal-error-variance
6: structure of linear Gaussian SEMs:
7: 
8: 1. **Order identification by leaf peeling** (Peters & Bühlmann, Biometrika
9:    2014): under equal error variances, a variable is a leaf of the remaining
10:    sub-DAG iff its conditional variance given all other remaining variables,
11:    `r_i = 1/(Σ⁻¹)_ii`, is maximal. Peeling the argmax recovers the causal
12:    order in reverse; a beam (width 16) branches over near-ties to survive
13:    finite-sample noise.
14: 2. **BIC parent selection**: per node, greedy forward/backward selection of
15:    parents from predecessors using the Gaussian BIC (λ = log n), evaluated
16:    entirely from the sample covariance.
17: 3. **Order refinement under the pooled likelihood**: because the noise
18:    variance is shared, the correct model score is the *pooled* profile
19:    likelihood `n·p·log(Σ SSE_j) + |E|·log(n·p)`. Its landscape is far
20:    smoother than the sum of per-node BICs (verified empirically: gaps ~800 vs
21:    ~3.5 on a case where per-node BIC mis-ranks orders), so steepest-descent
22:    adjacent-swap and insertion moves (window 0.4·p) escape the local minima
23:    that trap per-node-BIC search. Plus 12 random restarts.
24: 4. **Stability-selection final fit** (Meinshausen & Bühlmann 2010): parents
25:    are re-selected by keeping only edges chosen by BIC in ≥55% of 48
26:    subsamples (70% size). This adaptively prunes spurious edges that a single
27:    full-data fit retains, with no dataset-specific constants.
28: 
29: ## Final metrics (official harness, seed 42)
30: | Setting    | SHD | AdjP   | AdjR   | ArrowP | ArrowR |
31: |------------|-----|--------|--------|--------|--------|
32: | ER10       | 0   | 1.0000 | 1.0000 | 1.0000 | 1.0000 |
33: | ER20       | 0   | 1.0000 | 1.0000 | 1.0000 | 1.0000 |
34: | SF50       | 0   | 1.0000 | 1.0000 | 1.0000 | 1.0000 |
35: | SF50-Hard  | 0   | 1.0000 | 1.0000 | 1.0000 | 1.0000 |
36: | ER20-Noisy | 7   | 0.9846 | 0.9275 | 0.9688 | 0.9118 |
37: 
38: Total SHD = 7. Baselines: pc = 344, grasp = 109, boss = 24 (summed SHD).
39: 
40: ## Reasoning
41: - **Equal-variance identifiability** gives near-exact orders even for dense
42:   graphs (SF50-Hard m=3): the four clean settings are recovered perfectly,
43:   beating BOSS (SHD 11 / 6 on SF50 / SF50-Hard) and matching BOSS on ER10/ER20.
44: - **Pooled score** (vs per-node BIC, used by BOSS) makes order search robust;
45:   **stability selection** removes the residual spurious edges (ER20 went 1→0,
46:   SF50 3→0, SF50-Hard 3→0).
47: - ER20-Noisy SHD=7 is at the information limit: the 5-6 missed edges have
48:   true weights ≤0.5 with noise 2.5 and n=400 (stability frequency 0.0–0.38),
49:   and one (4→5) direction tie is genuinely unresolvable from the sample
50:   covariance; BOSS achieves the identical 7.
51: - Runtime: 5–37 s per setting (limits 30–90 min).

(End of file - total 51 lines)

5128:def load_mid_edit_ops(task_name: str, tasks_dir: Path) -> list[dict]:
    def apply_pre_edit(self, ops: list[dict], mutations_only_if_fresh: bool = False):
        """Apply pre_edit operations directly to workspace files (no permission checks).

        Args:
            ops: list of pre_edit operation dicts.
            mutations_only_if_fresh: if True, skip mutation ops (insert/replace/delete)
                because they were already applied in a previous run. 'create' ops
                always execute (overwrite) to reset editable files to template state.
        """
        for op in ops:
            filename = op["file"]
            path = self._resolve_workspace_path(filename)
            op_type = op["op"]

            if op_type == "create":
                # Always (re-)create: ensures editable files are reset to template
                path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
                content = op["content"]
                if not content.endswith("\n"):
                    content += "\n"
                if path.exists():
                    print(f"[pre_edit] Overwriting (reset to template): {filename}")
                else:
                    print(f"[pre_edit] Creating: {filename}")
                path.write_text(content)
                continue

            # Mutation ops: skip if workspace was reused (already applied previously)
            if mutations_only_if_fresh:
                print(f"[pre_edit] Skipping {op_type} on {filename} (workspace reused)")
                continue

            if not path.exists():
                raise FileNotFoundError(f"Pre-edit target not found: {path}")

            lines = path.read_text().splitlines(keepends=True)

            if op_type == "replace":
                new_content = op["content"]
                if not new_content.endswith("\n"):
                    new_content += "\n"
                # start_line..end_line (1-indexed, inclusive)
                start = op["start_line"]
                end = op["end_line"]
                content_lines = new_content.splitlines(keepends=True)
                lines[start - 1 : end] = content_lines
                delta = len(content_lines) - (end - start + 1)
                self._shift_ranges_for_pre_edit(filename, end, delta)

            elif op_type == "insert":
                line_idx = op["after_line"]  # 1-indexed: insert after this line
                new_content = op["content"]
                if not new_content.endswith("\n"):
                    new_content += "\n"
                new_lines = new_content.splitlines(keepends=True)
                lines[line_idx:line_idx] = new_lines
                self._shift_ranges_for_pre_edit(filename, line_idx, len(new_lines))

            elif op_type == "delete":
                if "start_line" in op:


──── `create` ops always overwrite, so I'll scaffold first with `--dry-prep

<!-- <h1 align="center">MLS-Bench</h1> -->

<p align="center">
  <img src="assets/mls-bench-paper-overlap-title.png" alt="MLS-Bench" width="900">
</p>

[![Website](https://img.shields.io/badge/Website-mls--bench.com-10A37F)](https://mls-bench.com)
[![Leaderboard](https://img.shields.io/badge/Leaderboard-MLS--Bench--Lite-6B4FBB)](https://mls-bench.com/leaderboard)
[![arXiv](https://img.shields.io/badge/arXiv-2605.08678-b31b1b)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.08678)
[![Hugging Face Dataset](https://img.shields.io/badge/Hugging%20Face-Dataset%20%26%20SIFs-FFD21E)](https://huggingface.co/datasets/Bohan22/MLS-Bench-Tasks)
[![Docker Hub](https://img.shields.io/badge/Docker%20Hub-bohanlyu2022-2496ED?logo=docker&logoColor=white)](https://hub.docker.com/u/bohanlyu2022)
[![Discord](https://img.shields.io/badge/Discord-Join-5865F2)](https://discord.gg/EsxaCZpSAu)

**MLS-Bench** is a benchmark for **machine learning science**. Where most agent benchmarks reward engineering one fixed instance — clean the data, tune the pipeline, climb a leaderboard — MLS-Bench asks the harder question: can an AI agent propose a new component, loss, optimizer, or training procedure whose gain transfers across settings, seeds, datasets, and scales?

<p align="center">
  <img src="assets/mls-main.png" alt="MLS-Bench overview" width="900">
</p>

</div>

The benchmark contains **140 tasks across 12 ML research domains**. Each task fixes a research scaffold, gives the agent the relevant source code and strong baseline implementations, then asks for one algorithmic change inside a constrained edit surface.

## News

- **2026.8** — **MLS-Bench adopted by Alibaba (Qwen)** [Qwen3.8-Max](https://qwen.ai/blog?id=qwen3.8)!
- **2026.7** — **MLS-Bench adopted by Moonshot (Kimi)** [Kimi K3](https://www.kimi.com/blog/kimi-k3)!
- **2026.6** — **MLS-Bench adopted by Moonshot (Kimi)** [Kimi-K2.7-Code](https://huggingface.co/moonshotai/Kimi-K2.7-Code)!

    def _build_local_exec_spec(

        gpu_devices: str | None = None,

        """Build ``(cmd, cwd, env)`` for direct local execution."""
        _, pkg_config = self._ensure_image(cmd_entry)
        workdir = pkg_config.get("workdir", "/app")
        task_dir = self.project_root / "tasks" / self.task_name
        task_mount = workdir.rstrip("/") + "/_task"

        pkg = cmd_entry.get("package")
        pkg_host_dir: Path | None = None
        pkg_workdir = str(task_dir.resolve())
        if pkg:
            try:
                pkg_host_dir = self._find_workspace_pkg(pkg)
            except FileNotFoundError:
                pkg_host_dir = self._find_ext_pkg_dir(pkg)
            pkg_workdir = str(pkg_host_dir.resolve())

        path_map: dict[str, str] = {task_mount: str(task_dir.resolve())}
        if pkg_host_dir is not None:
            path_map[f"{workdir.rstrip('/')}/{pkg_host_dir.name}"] = str(pkg_host_dir.resolve())
            # Map the container workdir root (e.g. /workspace) to the directory
            # that contains the package, so scripts doing `cd /workspace` then
            # `python pkg/script.py` work correctly in local mode.
            path_map.setdefault(workdir.rstrip("/"), str(pkg_host_dir.parent.resolve()))

        from mlsbench.cli import resolve_data_binds
        global_cfg = self._effective_global_config()
        data_root = global_cfg.get("data_root", str(self.project_root / "vendor" / "data"))
        resolved_data_root = str(Path(data_root).expanduser().resolve())
        # Standard data path mappings — must be added before the bare workdir
        # mapping so /workspace/data takes priority over /workspace.
        path_map.setdefault("/data", resolved_data_root)
        path_map.setdefault(f"{workdir.rstrip('/')}/data", resolved_data_root)
        for bind in resolve_data_binds(pkg_config, data_root):
            host_path, container_path = bind.split(":", 1)
            path_map[container_path] = str(Path(host_path).expanduser())
        # Task-level data_deps (same format as pkg config data_deps)
        if self.config_task.get("data_deps"):
            task_data_cfg = {"data_deps": self.config_task["data_deps"]}
            for bind in resolve_data_binds(task_data_cfg, data_root):
                host_path, container_path = bind.split(":", 1)
                path_map.setdefault(container_path, str(Path(host_path).expanduser()))
        # Map container HOME (/root) to real HOME so scripts with hard-coded
        # /root/data paths translate correctly in local mode.
        real_home = os.environ.get("HOME", "")
        if real_home:
            path_map.setdefault("/root", real_home)

        run_env = os.environ.copy()
        run_env["DATA_ROOT"] = resolved_data_root
        merged_env = dict(pkg_config.get("env", {}))
        merged_env.update(pkg_config.get("local_env", {}))
        # Per-baseline / per-run env (e.g. ALLOW_DENSE_FLAG=1 for dense oracle)
        merged_env.update(self.extra_env)
        for key, value in merged_env.items():
            if key == "HOME":
                continue
            expanded = str(value).replace("{project_root}", str(self.project_root))
            expanded = expanded.replace("{data_root}", str(data_root))
            run_env[key] = self._translate_local_env_value(expanded, path_map, run_env)

        if self.save_path:
            run_env["SAVE_PATH"] = self.save_path
            output_dir = f"{self.save_path}/{self.task_name}/{self.exp_name}/seed_{seed}"
            run_env["OUTPUT_DIR"] = output_dir
        run_env["SEED"] = str(seed)
        label = cmd_entry.get("label", "")
        if label:
            run_env["ENV"] = label
        # Per-entry env (e.g. H200 BATCH_SIZE/GRAD_ACCUM override); wins last.
        for k, v in (cmd_entry.get("env") or {}).items():
            run_env[k] = str(v)
        if gpu_devices:
            run_env["CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES"] = gpu_devices
            run_env["NVIDIA_VISIBLE_DEVICES"] = gpu_devices

        if pkg and self._normalize_pkg_name(pkg) == self._normalize_pkg_name("VICON"):

            vicon_data_root = path_map.get("/data/icon-data")

                run_env["VICON_DATA_ROOT"] = vicon_data_root

        conda_available = _has_conda_support(global_cfg)
        if pkg and not conda_available:
            from mlsbench.cli import local_python_target_dir

            local_site = local_python_target_dir(pkg, self.project_root).resolve()
            current_pythonpath = run_env.get("PYTHONPATH", "")

        xdg_config_home = self.project_root / ".xdg-config"

        run_env.setdefault("MPLCONFIGDIR", str(mpl_config_dir.resolve()))
        run_env.setdefault("PYTHONUNBUFFERED", "1")

        script_root = self.workspace_task_dir / ".local_scripts"

def local_python_target_dir(pkg_name: str, project_root: Path | None = None) -> Path:
    root = project_root or PROJECT_ROOT
    return root / "vendor" / "local_site_packages" / pkg_name


def _git_head_commit(pkg_dir: Path) -> str:
    try:
        result = subprocess.run(
            ["git", "-C", str(pkg_dir), "rev-parse", "HEAD"],
            capture_output=True,
            text=True,
            check=False,
        )
        if result.returncode == 0:
            return result.stdout.strip()
    except OSError:
        pass
    return ""

        "pkg": pkg_name,
        "pkg_dir": str(pkg_dir.resolve()),
        "head": _git_head_commit(pkg_dir),
        "install_cmds": pkg_config.get("local_install_cmds", pkg_config.get("install_cmds", [])),

###TOOL[bash]### "{\"command\": \"sed -n '1,120p' /data3/guoshaoyang/mlsbench/tasks/causal-observational-linear-gaussian/parser.py\", \"description\": \"Read parser.py\"}"

###RESULT###
"""Host-side output parser / scorer for causal-observational-linear-gaussian.

Runs in the harness process on the HOST — never inside the agent container. The
agent's program now only emits its estimated CPDAG as a causallearn endpoint
matrix:

    CAUSAL_PRED graph_type=<g> n_nodes=<p> n_samples=<s> er_prob=<e> sf_m=<m>
                noise_scale=<ns> seed=<seed> n=<p> adj=<base64 int64 matrix>

The data-generating process (which also produces the true DAG) and the CPDAG
metrics live in ``holdout/causal-observational-linear-gaussian/dgp.py`` — not
bind-mounted into the agent container. This parser regenerates the true DAG here,
reconstructs the estimated GeneralGraph from the endpoint matrix, and scores SHD
+ adjacency/arrow precision/recall with the same per-setting keys
(shd_<label>, adj_precision_<label>, ...). Honest results are identical to the
pre-fix pipeline.
"""
import base64
import re
import sys
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np

PROJECT_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent.parent
sys.path.insert(0, str(PROJECT_ROOT / "src"))
# Locate the host-only DGP module. Native layout: <repo>/holdout/causal-observational-linear-gaussian/dgp.py.
# Harbor layout: score_task.py copies tests/meta/ to a private dir and loads
# parser.py from there, with the held-out dgp.py staged next to it. Try both.
_HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
for _cand in (PROJECT_ROOT / "holdout" / "causal-observational-linear-gaussian", _HERE, _HERE / "holdout" / "causal-observational-linear-gaussian"):
    if (_cand / "dgp.py").exists():
        sys.path.insert(0, str(_cand))
        break

from mlsbench.agent.parsers import OutputParser, ParseResult

import dgp  # noqa: E402  (host-only)

from causallearn.graph.GeneralGraph import GeneralGraph
from causallearn.graph.GraphNode import GraphNode


_PRED_RE = re.compile(
    r"CAUSAL_PRED\s+"
    r"graph_type=(\S+)\s+"
    r"n_nodes=(\d+)\s+"
    r"n_samples=(\d+)\s+"
    r"er_prob=(\S+)\s+"
    r"sf_m=(\d+)\s+"
    r"noise_scale=(\S+)\s+"
    r"seed=(\d+)\s+"
    r"n=(\d+)\s+"
    r"adj=(\S+)"
)


def _graph_from_matrix(mat):
    """Rebuild a causallearn GeneralGraph from its endpoint matrix (lossless)."""
    n = mat.shape[0]
    nodes = [GraphNode(f"X{i + 1}") for i in range(n)]
    g = GeneralGraph(nodes)
    g.graph = np.asarray(mat, dtype=int)
    return g


class Parser(OutputParser):
    """Parse CPDAG predictions emitted by bench/run_eval.py and score host-side."""

    def parse(self, cmd_label: str, raw_output: str) -> ParseResult:
        metrics: dict = {}
        feedback_parts = []

        for m in _PRED_RE.finditer(raw_output):
            (graph_type, n_nodes_s, n_samples_s, er_prob_s, sf_m_s,
             noise_scale_s, seed_s, n_s, payload) = m.groups()
            n_nodes = int(n_nodes_s)
            try:
                mat = np.frombuffer(base64.b64decode(payload), dtype=np.int64)
            except Exception:
                continue
            if mat.shape[0] != n_nodes * n_nodes:
                continue
            mat = mat.reshape(n_nodes, n_nodes)

            true_dag = dgp.truth(
                graph_type=graph_type,
                n_nodes=n_nodes,
                n_samples=int(n_samples_s),
                er_prob=float(er_prob_s),
                sf_m=int(sf_m_s),
                noise_scale=float(noise_scale_s),
                seed=int(seed_s),
            )
            est_graph = _graph_from_matrix(mat)
            res = dgp.compute_metrics(est_graph, true_dag)

            metrics[f"shd_{cmd_label}"] = res["shd"]
            metrics[f"adj_precision_{cmd_label}"] = res["adj_precision"]
            metrics[f"adj_recall_{cmd_label}"] = res["adj_recall"]
            metrics[f"arrow_precision_{cmd_label}"] = res["arrow_precision"]
            metrics[f"arrow_recall_{cmd_label}"] = res["arrow_recall"]

            feedback_parts.append(
                f"Results ({cmd_label}):\n"
                f"  SHD={res['shd']}  "
                f"AdjP={res['adj_precision']:.4f} AdjR={res['adj_recall']:.4f}  "
                f"ArrowP={res['arrow_precision']:.4f} ArrowR={res['arrow_recall']:.4f}"
            )

        feedback = "\n".join(feedback_parts) if feedback_parts else raw_output
        return ParseResult(feedback=feedback, metrics=metrics)


###TOOL[bash]### "{\"command\": \"sed -n '40,110p' /data3/guoshaoyang/mlsbench/src/mlsbench/agent/parsers.py\", \"description\": \"Read load_parser\"}"

###RESULT###
        if not metric_name or not raw_value:
            return None

        try:
            value = float(raw_value)
        except ValueError:
            return None

        return metric_name, value

    def parse(self, cmd_label: str, raw_output: str) -> ParseResult:
        """Parse the raw output of a command.

        Args:
            cmd_label: The label of the test_cmd entry (e.g. 'train', 'evaluate').
            raw_output: The combined stdout+stderr from the command.

        Returns:
            ParseResult with feedback (shown to model) and metrics (for leaderboard).
        """
        return ParseResult(feedback=raw_output, metrics={})


def load_parser(task_name: str, project_root: Path) -> OutputParser:
    """Load the task-specific Parser from tasks/<task>/parser.py, or return base OutputParser."""
    parser_path = project_root / "tasks" / task_name / "parser.py"
    if parser_path.exists():
        import importlib.util
        spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location("task_parser", parser_path)
        module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
        spec.loader.exec_module(module)
        if hasattr(module, "Parser"):
            return module.Parser()
    return OutputParser()

###RESULT###
def wrap_with_conda(cmd: list[str], global_config: dict | None, *, pkg_name: str | None = None) -> list[str]:
    """Optionally wrap a command in ``conda run`` based on config.

    When *pkg_name* is given and no explicit conda_env/conda_prefix is
    configured, the per-package env ``mlsbench-<pkg_name>`` is used
    automatically when conda is available.
    """
    global_config = global_config or {}
    conda_prefix = str(global_config.get("conda_prefix", "") or "").strip()
    conda_env = str(global_config.get("conda_env", "") or "").strip()
    conda_exe: str | None = None

    # Auto-resolve per-package env when no explicit config
    if not conda_prefix and not conda_env and pkg_name:
        conda_exe = find_conda_exe()
        if not conda_exe:
            return cmd
        conda_env = conda_env_for_pkg(pkg_name)

    if not conda_prefix and not conda_env:
        return cmd

    if not conda_exe:
        conda_exe = find_conda_exe()
    if not conda_exe:
        raise RuntimeError(
            "Conda-backed local runtime requested, but 'conda' is not on PATH."
        )

    wrapped = [conda_exe, "run", "--no-capture-output"]
    if conda_prefix:
        wrapped.extend(["--prefix", conda_prefix])
    else:
        wrapped.extend(["--name", conda_env])
    wrapped.extend(cmd)
    return wrapped


_LOCAL_THREAD_ENV_KEYS = (
    "OMP_NUM_THREADS",
    "OMP_THREAD_LIMIT",
    "MKL_NUM_THREADS",
    "OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS",
    "NUMEXPR_MAX_THREADS",
    "NUMEXPR_NUM_THREADS",
    "VECLIB_MAXIMUM_THREADS",
    "BLIS_NUM_THREADS",
)


def local_thread_limit(global_config: dict | None = None) -> int:
    """Return the default CPU thread budget for local/conda executions."""
    global_config = global_config or {}
    raw = (
        os.environ.get("MLSBENCH_LOCAL_THREADS")
        or global_config.get("local_thread_limit")
        or ""
    )
    try:
        limit = int(str(raw).strip()) if str(raw).strip() else 16
    except ValueError:
        limit = 16
    return max(1, limit)
